# Function Port Tracking

Single source of truth for function-to-function port progress.
One original function = one row. Update this file in the same commit as
the code/doc it describes.

## Protocol

Per-function loop (see `docs/12-function-map.md` §3):
1. Analyze `analysis/pseudo/<file>.c` + xrefs → entry in
   `docs/12-functions/<subsystem>.md` (signature, semantics, constants,
   edge cases, globals/assets touched, verification method) → status `[~]`
2. Port 1:1 to `port/src/f/<name>.c` with `/* orig: 0xNNNNNNN */`
3. Verify (unit selftest for pure fns; headless-run + DOSBox diff for
   stateful fns) → status `[x]`
4. Rename in Ghidra → update the Name column

Status legend:
- `[ ]` pending
- `[~]` analyzed (doc entry written, not yet ported)
- `[x]` ported + verified (evidence path in Notes)
- `[-]` intentionally not ported (e.g. DOS4GW bookkeeping replaced by
  `port/src/sys/` — note what replaces it)

Rules:
- Work strictly in order: top-to-bottom within a level, L0 → L19
  (call-graph depth; a function may only be ported after all its callees
  are `[x]` or `[-]`).
- Notes must be short: pointer to the subsystem doc section and/or the
  verification evidence.
- After each batch, refresh the **Progress** counts below.

| 2026-07-13 | **0x013c4d (L13, 1809 B) PORTED -- the actor hit test (26 checks).** Unblocked by decoding the box source: the eight hit/hurt arrays live in the per-type sprite struct `g_dat_639ec[type]` at +0x14..+0x30 and hold heap offsets, the same convention fn_013908 already uses. Findings: **the intangible window is half-open** (`word[+4] <= frame < word[+6]` rejects; a frame *below* word[+4] still hits); **the Y gate returns 0 through 0x0001434f, skipping the facing fix-up** that the other rejections fall through to; **the overlap test is corners-or-straddle on each axis**, so a larger actor swallows a smaller one; **byte(rec+off+0x44) is signed through bit 7** ((v&0x7f)-0x80) and is then clamped into the victim's X span; the pair record (EBX) supplies A's frame divisor while B's own animation record supplies B's, and `B[0x4a]` is compared as a heap-relative 32-bit value like the original's flat address. Test traps hit: A's frame index must be re-set after the reaction tests (they zero A[0x11]), and a 200 in a byte field is a *negative* offset, which is what made the clamp probe read 80. Frontier note: 0x03209c/0x0320f5/0x03ed6f/0x0445c9 are all wrappers whose callees (0x012616, 0x01441e, 0x03e20f, 0x03af18) are still pending, so 0x01441e -> 0x013c4d is now the live chain; scratch/xrefs.py + scratch/cals.py give caller/callee lists (code VA = file offset + 0x2600). Suite 2591 -> **2617 PASS 0 FAIL** (2 runs + ASan clean). TOTALS 506 -> **507/528**.

| 2026-07-13 | **REAL STAGE 1 LOADS END-TO-END: `fn_03690b` (master data) -> `fn_037b03(0)` -> `fn_0376c8` (hero spawn) now run against the shipped `NIGHT.RES` -- 29 new checks (`test_real_master_load`), and the long-open heap bug is FIXED.** **Heap fix (was under-provisioning by 4 bytes, open since the heap was ported):** `heap_alloc` (0x042ada) requires `B_SIZE >= floor4(size+7)` -- `ADD EDX,7` then `AND DL,0xfc` -- but `heap_acquire_region` sized the new region's data area as `round4(size)`, *smaller than the request that triggered it*, so the acquire-region->retry path ALWAYS failed and any malloc without existing slack returned 0 silently (loaders don't check). Now sized with heap_alloc's own rounding -> allocations up to 8 MB succeed (probe). DPMI substitution stays faithful: the original grows a whole DPMI block, so a fresh region always exceeds a request. Two heap unit tests updated to the corrected sizes (0x2c+0x1004 / 0x1004 coalesce / 0x1004 no-split); a third (`fn_0100d9: region full before free`) had been passing *because of the bug* and now states its intent with `heap_arena_cap_set(0x300)` (setter no longer clamps below 0x40). **Second finding: the record cache (DAT_63e14/63e18/63e1c/63e20) is cross-test state.** Once allocations succeeded, `fn_03e0a0` really appended record nodes and `fn_03ee0a`'s unlink walk followed stale links into unrelated arena bytes -> wild write/SIGBUS. Stage tests now `cache_reset()`/`cache_restore()`. Latent original bug recorded: `fn_03e14c` unlinks with an UNGUARDED write through neighbour links, so removing the last node writes through offset 0 (DS:0x51/0x55 in the original). Verified real facts: stage1.wal rooms **4**; room chain + DAT_63354 set; st1s.spr loaded (header 7/99); stage1.stg records **88**; night1.chr entry 0 word **100**; stage 1 fills type slots 01(SA) 09(**A only**) 0b 1f 20 24 25 29 2a 2e -- so sprite/action pairing is NOT guaranteed by real data (fixture-only assumption); hero entity spawned (DAT_53c7c/53c84). Master load: key counts gun.key **10** (leading blank lines skipped) / catch.key **2**, gun.act **192** / gun1.act **196** records, sprite structs pause 10 / system 11 / system2 13 / over 7 / face 92 / gage 3 / gun **266** / mark 76 / dust 16, all 100 .voc slots, exptab.dat rows parse through the numeric text loader. (b) **Test hygiene:** the AI-handler tests must not call heap_arena_init() -- stage globals (DAT_63e14/18/1c/20, g_dat_639ec[], g_dat_53818[]) cache heap OFFSETS allocated by earlier real-resource tests, so re-initializing the arena mid-suite turns them into dangling offsets that later teardowns free. | 2026-07-13 | **0x03c231 (L12, 1066 B) PORTED + 1 test (9 checks) -- THE INTERLACED FILL BLIT, and a new keystone: it is the only unported callee of 0x012efa (the entity frame dispatcher).** ABI `ret 0x10`: **EAX = dest linear address, EDX = col, EBX = row, ECX = width (run clip bound); stack: row count, RLE stream, flip byte, fill byte.** It is a *fill* blitter (not a copy): every run is painted with one byte through fn_041e20, i.e. it draws a solid silhouette (mask/shape) rather than sprite pixels -- the sibling of the mode!=0 arm of fn_03bb42. Distinct behaviours, all pinned by tests: (1) **interlace** -- the row loop runs `rows << 1` iterations and only the EVEN ones draw, the odd ones consume a stream row unseen (`n = *p; p += 1; for (j<n) p += p[1] + 2`), while the destination still advances by a full 0x140 per drawn row; (2) forward clipping `left = -col` / `width = 0x140 - col` with three arms (pre-clip, post-clip, plain) and the clipped fill anchored at `dest + left`, not `dest + col_acc`; (3) **mirrored mode** anchors the destination at `dest + row*0x140 + col + width - 1` and walks right-to-left (`dest - col_acc - len + 1`), its own clip rule being `left = col + width - 0x140` **else** `width += col` when col < 0 -- and runs shorter than the overflow vanish completely (test: nothing drawn at col 0x130 width 0x20); (4) guards col>0x140 / col+width<0 / row>0xc8 / row+rows<0 abort everything, and rows==0 draws nothing; (5) the negative-row skip reuses fn_03bb42's quirky `n=*p; p+=2; for(j<n) p += *p + 2; p -= 1`, which only lands on the next stream row when the first run's gap byte is 0 (latent original arithmetic, kept faithfully). Stream row format is the same as fn_03bb42: `[n]` + n x `[gap][len][len filler bytes]`; the filler is never read here but must be present for cursor advance. **Test-authoring note: the stream must be ONE contiguous buffer** -- passing three separate static arrays made the 2nd drawn row read past the first array (silent garbage, caught by the interlace check). Probe kept: scratch/fill_blit_probe.c. Unblocks 0x012efa (1428 B, entity frame dispatcher) + 0x03801b chain. | 2026-07-13 | **0x043a7f (L5, 544 B) PORTED + 1 test (12 checks) -- the format-string scanner that drives the whole emit/parse family; L5 now has a single function left (0x0461cc).** It is *not* printf: it is the scanf-like mini-language used by the command/stream layer. ABI: EAX=ctx (next()/emit() callbacks, count at +0xc, flags at +0x10 -- the same layout as cmd_ctx, cast where a callee wants it), EDX=format string, EBX=pos (the position-stream cursor passed to converters as their EDX). Entry clears the stop flag (flags &= ~2). Per format char: (a) if the class of the NEXT char (kCharClass[(c+1)&0xff]) has bit 0x02, cmdline_count() consumes a word and adds to the running char total; (b) a literal must be equal to what ctx->next() returns -- a mismatch putbacks via ctx->emit() unless the stop flag is set, then the scan ends; (c) '%' runs cmdline_parse() over the flag/width part and dispatches: e/f/g/E/F/G -> fn_0440a9, d -> fn_044387(10,sign), i -> (0,sign) auto base, o -> (8,sign), u -> (10,no sign), x/X/p -> (16,sign), c -> emit_chars, n -> emit_value(ctx,pos,chars_so_far), s -> fn_043e34, '[' -> fn_043fc3 with EBX = the address of the format cursor (the original pushes its own stack slot), '%' -> a literal percent; any other conversion char is ignored without being consumed. A converter returning <= 0 ends the scan; otherwise the char total grows and the conversion counter only increments when ctx flags bit 0 is set. When the stop flag is set the loop exits, but a trailing '%n' is still honoured (0x43c56-7b). Return = conversion count, or -1 when none happened with the stop flag set. Observed original behaviour worth knowing: a space inside the format counts a *stream* word, so "%d %d" against "10 20" performs two conversions and advances two slots while only the first value is stored (the second conversion runs against the eaten word/exhausted stream) -- reproduced, not fixed. | 2026-07-08 | **CONFIRMED LATENT BUG in fn_048f4c (0x048f4c, fx128 poly-eval core) + 0x0487b0 port BLOCKED on it. Dedicated defect-analysis pass (no C change this turn).** **The bug:** `fn_048f4c`'s loop calls the multiply at 0x048f73 (real `0x047d4e`/fx_mul64) and MUST feed the multiply's *returned* ESI into the subsequent `ROL ESI,16`/`MOV SI,arr[i+1].exp`/`ROL`/`0x04794a` dance (ground-truth 0x048f4c is 32 insns: `...CALL 0x047d4e; LEA EDI,[EDI+0xa]; MOV EBX/ECX,[EDI]; ROL ESI,16; MOV SI,[EDI+8]; ROL ESI,16; CALL 0x04794a; DEC [EBP+0xc]; JNZ`). `0x047d4e`'s epilogue is `MOV ESI,ECX` (it **returns the normalized exponent in ESI**, clobbering it). But the C port `fn_048f4c` uses `fx_mul64(...)` (returns only lo:hi, no exp) and then does `ESI = rol16(ESI)` on the *carried* ESI instead of the multiply's returned exp. **Proof:** 20,000/20,000 random (a,b,esi) inputs give `post-rd4e ESI` divergence between the C-port model (rol16'd carried) and the real `0x047d4e`-returned exp (e.g. carried `0xf61401ea` vs real `0x0xf3ff`). **The Python ref `fp_048422_ref.py::f048f4c` has the IDENTICAL bug** (`ESI = rol16(ESI)` after rd4e, not `rol16(r[2])`), so the C `0x048422`/`0x04836c` tests agree *wrongly* against each other (both mirror the bug). **EXACT FIX (both languages):** after the multiply in f4c, capture its returned exp — C: `fx64_t o; fn_047d4e(EAX,EDX,mant_lo,mant_hi,ESI,&o); EAX=o.lo;EDX=o.hi; ESI=o.exp;` *then* `ROL`; Python: `ESI = rol16(r[2])` (where `r=rd4e(...)`). Note `0x04794a` **preserves** ESI (input-only; C `fx_04794a.c` treats esi as an in/out local and returns u64 lo:hi — the earlier `disassemble_function(0x04794a)` output was Ghidra's WRONG 228-insn merged fn, so `MOV ESI,EBP` there is not real). Entry ESI high16 is irrelevant (washed out by the `MOV SI,[EBP+8]` at 0x48f66). **VERIFIED fix sanity (scratch/check_f4c_fix_sanity.py, monkeypatched ref):** corrected `0x048422` = 12/14 frozen vectors UNCHANGED; the 2 that differ are the threshold *boundary* cases v6 `lo==th` and v7 `hi==th`, which become **input-identity** — exactly matching their "boundary / no-op" test-comment intent, i.e. the frozen v6/v7 values were the buggy ones. The corrected `f4c` is a faithful mirror of the real 32-insn 0x048f4c, so these are the true values. **BLAST RADIUS / fix checklist (dedicated pass):** (1) fix `fn_048f4c` in f.c (use `fn_047d4e`+`out.exp`); (2) fix `test_fn_048f4c` hand-sequence to use `fn_047d4e`'s returned exp; (3) fix `fp_048422_ref.py::f048f4c` (`rol16(r[2])`); (4) regenerate the hardcoded `test_fn_048422` vectors (2 of 14 change: v6/v7) AND `test_fn_04836c` vectors (17, via fn_048422 normalization) from the corrected ref; (5) rebuild → suite green. **0x0487b0 (L2, 464B) CANNOT be ported until f4c is fixed** — it calls f4c twice and consumes f4c's *final* ESI (f4c's final ESI is currently wrong because of this bug). **0x0487b0 complete register/stack trace DONE this turn** (phases in the prior 0x0487b0 spec entry; key correction from this pass): ESI is a **clean C local** — r94a preserves it, only rb79/rd4e/f4c return/rewrite it. Because r94a preserves ESI, the ESI pushed at 0x04882b is the **constant 0xbffe3ffe** in BOTH the small and large domain branches, and at 0x04884a the ESI is `0x3ffe3ffd` — so no "clobber re-derivation" is needed. The domain branch is a 64-bit lexicographic compare `(a->hi:a->lo) <= (0xb504f333:0xf9de6484)` (the `[EDI+8]==0x3ffe` word-cmp at 0x0487ea is always true so only hi/lo matter); small-branch writes a={t1}, DEC's the flagword, large-branch leaves a and recomputes t1. The ONLY new helper: expose f4c's final ESI (optional `uint32_t *out_final_esi` on `fn_048f4c`). **CAVEAT (Ghidra):** `scratch/dis_0487b0.txt` = `disassemble_function(0x0487b0)`; its 0x0487b0–0x048980 range is CORRECT (matches a raw byte dump) but the embedded 0x048f4c/0x047b79 sub-ranges are Ghidra's wrong merged functions (they show unrelated 0x04a400/0x049d9a/0x04a880 code) — use the C ports (source of truth) or fresh `disassemble_function <addr>` for each callee. **r94a ref change committed:** `fp_04794a_ref.py` gained `emulate_full()` → (EAX,EDX,ESI); backward-compat `emulate()` (2-tuple) preserved. NEXT (fresh context): execute the f4c fix checklist above, then port 0x0487b0 against the FIXED f4c (trace already done). | STATUS.md L2 |
| 2026-07-08 | **VIDEO-RAM MODEL ESTABLISHED (subsystem decision) + 0x03c65b (L10, 143 B) PORTED -- playable render-path unblock.** The VGA video segment (flat 0xa0000, DAT_000a0000) was the last unmodeled subsystem blocking the render path (fn_0003fade/03f645/03f592/03a5f7/03f462 were no-ops; 0x03c65b video-flatten was blocked because node->start is a 0xa0000-based flat video addr with no base mapped). **Decision:** model the 64 KB video window [0xa0000,0xb0000) as an explicit `uint8_t g_video_ram[0x10000]`; flat video addr `va` maps to `g_video_ram[va-0xa0000]` via `video_map()` (out-of-window clamped to base, documented; real game stays in-window). Unblocks 0x03c65b + the blit family (0x040660/0x0406f5). **0x03c65b (48 insns)**: register-ABI EAX=a0 (packed-records dest). For i in 0..0xc7: cur=g_dat_62d68[i] (head), off=0; do { len=(int16)node->len@+8; mem_copy(out+off, video_map(node->start@+0), len); off+=len; cur=node->next@+4 } while (off<0x140); out+=0x140. Faithful transcription (do-while: copies at least one node, then fills to 0x140). Both callees (canary 0x2c, mem_copy 0x041e6c) ported. Test: 3 checks -- seed 200 lists (fn_034990, all heads start=0xa0000 len=0x140), pattern video RAM, flatten -> rec0/rec100/rec199 each == g_video_ram[0..0x13f] (verifies video_map + the full 200-list loop). So 0x0440a9 is GATED on the [0x53648] float-builder until that target is identified+ported. It is a complex multi-mode parser (int 0x10 / raw-64 0x20,0x40 / float-else; sign, decimal-point 2nd-pass shift, exponent e/E; register-ABI ctx=EAX,pos=EDX; ES: far/near output slot via *pos +=8/+4 then dest=*(old *pos); reuses the emit_ctx struct + the emit_value pos convention). (2) **0x0487b0 (L2,464 B), 0x048422 (L3,354 B, gates 0x04836c), 0x048c8c (L2,467 B)** are fx128 composites (call the fx128 cluster: 0x47d4e=fx_mul64, 0x47b79, 0x4791a, 0x4794a, 0x47f0e, 0x48f4c, 0x48f9e -- all ported). **KEY LESSON (refines the port-status method): a DIRECT-call-only callee scan (CALL 0x...) FALSELY declares a clean win when the function also has INDIRECT calls -- function-pointer globals (e.g. [0x53648], [0x63360]... no, [0x53648] specifically), ctx->next/emit, or `CALL [mem]`. Before porting, grep the FULL disassembly for `CALL` with a memory/register operand, not just `CALL 0x...`.** The fx128 composites still need the fx128 128-bit float format + bit-exact rounding understood before porting. | STATUS.md |

| 2026-07-08 | **fn_047f54 (0x047f54, 189B) — register-ABI fixed-point normalizer**: EAX:EDX (64-bit) in, EBX out {lo,hi,field}. Core: v=(hi<<32\|lo)<<11; eax/edx split; ecx=SAR(hi,20); cx=ecx&0x7ff. Zero → ecx=0. Output: out[0]=eax, out[1]=edx, out[2]=(cx<<1)\|bit31(ecx). The 0x0463dc call is a spurious interpreter thunk (EAX saved/restored via PUSH/POP, return discarded) → no-op standin. **CRITICAL REFERENCE BUG FIXED:** the SAR(hi,20) must be ARITHMETIC (sign-extending), not logical — for hi with bit31 set, ecx high 16 = 0xffff so bit31(ecx)=1 (sets the output field's LSB). The Python reference originally used `>>` (logical) and produced a wrong field LSB for negative inputs; corrected with ctypes.c_int32 arithmetic shift. 2009 differential vectors generated via scratch/fp_047f54_ref.py; all PASS. suite 817→818 PASS 0 FAIL. | STATUS.md L1 |

| 2026-07-08 | **0x485eb refined trace** (full 53-instruction disasm 0x485eb..0x48664 read). Corrects the earlier "two normalization loops" reading: it is a SINGLE shift-and-clamped-subtract loop with feedback edges. After `D=(exp_a&0x7fff)-(exp_b&0x7fff)`, if D<0 jump to L_other (0x4864a). Else one compare/subtract: `sub` (A=A-B, STC) reached iff A>=B (unsigned 64-bit); `skip` (A unchanged, CF=0) iff B>A. Then 0x48612: EDI+=CF (EDI=did-subtract), DEC ESI (D--), `JL 0x48627` if D<0. The loop body shifts A(EDX:EAX) left by 1 (ADD EAX,EAX;ADC EDX,EDX), then `JC 0x4860d` (A_hi overflow -> re-subtract B) or `JS 0x485ff` (A_hi top bit set -> re-compare B vs A) or back to 0x48612. So it shifts A left up to (mag_a-mag_b) times, clamped-subtracting B whenever A grows to >=B, counting subtractions in EDI — i.e. a division-like / fixed-point alignment, NOT a plain subtract. Epilogue 0x4864e-0x48665: `AND EBX,0x8000; OR ESI,EBX` (exp gets a sign bit), then `XOR BX,CX; JNS; NEG EDI` (EDI=sign 0/-1). HIGH RISK to hand-verify (no reference); defer for a dedicated pass with an original-binary trace. | STATUS.md L0 |

Suggested signature: uint64_t fn_04794a(uint32_t p1,uint32_t p2,uint32_t p3,uint32_t p4, uint16_t exp). Port + Python-ref + differential-verify (CARRY4/CARRY2/CONCAT->64-bit ops, as in 0x048e60). | 2026-07-08 | **0x04794a pass — decompile found BUGGY; emulator + 40k vectors built (C port deferred).** Built a data-driven mini-x86 emulator (`scratch/fp_04794a_ref.py`, 149 insns, CF/ZF/SF + 8/16/32-bit operand sizes, RCR/SHRD/SAR/etc.) from the ground-truth disasm, and 40,090 differential vectors (`scratch/fp_04794a_vectors.h`). Differential-testing a Ghidra-decompile-based C port (`scratch/fp_04794a_cport.c`) against the emulator shows ~49% mismatch. Hand-traced the DISASM for i=56 (EAX=1,EBX=1,ECX=0,EDX=0,ESI=0x20001): the alignment `SHRD EBX,EAX,1` puts the fractional bit (EAX bit0) into CF, but the very next `OR EDI,EBX` (0x047a0a) CLEARS CF, so the bit is LOST and the normalize loop's `ADC EAX,EAX` gives **2** (the true binary answer). Ghidra's decompile mis-models this (it keeps the SHRD carry into the normalize loop, giving 3). So the decompile is WRONG for this fn (a latent precision-loss quirk in the original FPU add), and the emulator (disasm-faithful) is the correct reference. C port deferred pending a careful disasm-faithful transcription (hand or emulator->C codegen); the decompile-based cport is superseded. This confirms: for intricate fixed-point fns, verify against the DISASM/emulator, not the decompile. | STATUS.md L0 |

Verified 20,000 random+edge vectors 100% match the emulator; the committed test ships 3,000 vectors (scratch/fp_04794a_ctest.h, force-tracked). Splits the int16 gauge words entity[+0x23]/[+0x29]: for the player (EAX==DAT_53c7c) /100 and %100 (signed idiv, NO bound on the colour-table index -- latent, kept); otherwise /200 and half-toward-zero %100 with the colour tables clamped to 0x95 past index 5 / 4. Empty panel (entity[+0x0d]==0 or entity[+0x56]==0): face.spr (DAT_53c8c) record [+0x0d] at (slot*0xaa+4, row 3) + gage.spr (DAT_63e00) record 0 at (slot*0xaa+0x13, row 0x1d) via fn_03bb42, then a 0x64x2 rect_fill (fn_04052c) when the quotient is non-zero, a second rect_fill of remainder width, and a 4-px 0xd0 vline_set (fn_040506) marker at column slot*0xaa+0x17+remainder. Armed panel: with 0 < entity[+0x0d] < 10 the split is REPLACED by entity[+0x56]/3, colour 0x9e and record index 1, then one gage blit at (0x20,0xb3) + one rect_fill at (0x20,0xbb). Struct fields are heap offsets (fn_03d9cf layout +4/+8/+0x34/+0x38); the second gage blit uses record 0 with NO offset-array indexing (kept). Adds g_blk_51c80 for the never-written DAT_51c90/51c91 colour bytes. Callers: 0x03801b. 9 checks | f.c |

Gates: byte B[0x30] inert flag; the intangible frame window word anim[+4]..anim[+6]; state 0x67/0x68 hits only its B[0x4a] partner; the |dy| <= byte(+0x56) Y gate (returns 0 with no facing fix-up). Boxes come from eight parallel dword arrays in g_dat_639ec[type] (+0x14/+0x18/+0x24/+0x28 for B, +0x1c/+0x20/+0x2c/+0x30 for A) indexed by the signed frame remainder, mirrored by A[0x1b]; overlap = corners-inside OR full straddle on both axes. Success: contact X = A[1] +/- byte(+0x44) (bit 7 = negative), clamped into B's span, spawn of a type-0xc9 effect actor through fn_010efc (state = byte +0x5d, +0x30 = 1, facing copied/flipped), then the player 0x65/0x99 reaction ladder (anim byte +0x6c -> 0xa1, else 0xab/0xa0 by player[0x27]) or the generic "turn B toward A" tail. See docs/19-hit-box-model.md | f.c, f.h, test_f1.c, docs/19 |

| [x] | 0x016b8f | 1116 | fn_016b8f | entity/move | THE THREE-PHASE STEP/RETURN WALK (approach -> pause -> walk back), a scripted creature movement. ABI: EAX=entity, EDX=step, EBX/ECX=caller comparison values, plus TWO stack out params at [ebp+0x10]/[ebp+0x14] (stack args 3-4; args 1-2 unused). Always writes [e+0x17]=2; when [e+0x8f]==0 it re-aims (mid-Y from DAT_63354, [e+0x9f] from ECX, [e+0x9b] from fn_01519f vs EBX, phase/accumulators cleared). Phase 0 walks while abs([e+0xa3]) < ([e+0x11f]>>1)+0xf with out_a negated when [e+0x9b]<0 and out_b negated when [e+0x9f]>=0 (opposite rule, straight from the machine code); [e+0x97]<0 = out of budget -> out params written, return 1, no decrement. Phase 1 forces out_b=0, accumulates only [e+0xa3], and (only if [e+0x9f]!=0) aborts when (EBX,ECX) leaves the [e+0xb3]+-0x28 / [e+0xb7]+-7 box -> [e+0x8f]=0, return 1. Phase 2 flips the [e+0x9f] polarity and NEVER accumulates [e+0xa7] (latent orig quirk, kept). Unblocks 20 pending functions. |

| [x] | 0x03f15f | 606 | fn_03f15f | **THE VESA MODE SET (mode 0x101 = 640x480x8) -- fills every geometry global the blit family reads.** Stack probe (PUSH 0x54 / CALL 0x0418ee), seg_save (fn_041df2), INT 0x31 AX=0x0100 allocates 0x21 DOS code paragraphs (the selector is stored in the block's first word, buffer = block+2), INT 0x10 AX=0x4F00 through fn_00103e0; the reply must start with "VESA" (DS 0x1728) or "VBE2" (DS 0x172d) (mem_cmp 0x0429a8) else the banner "\nYour Vga Card Not Support VesaMode!!!\n" (DS 0x1732) via fn_042394 and return 0. Free the code seg (AX=0x0101), allocate 0x11 DOS DATA paragraphs, INT 0x10 AX=0x4F01 CX=0x101 = ModeInfoBlock. ModeAttributes low byte &3==3 -> DAT_63e58=0, &5==5 -> DAT_63e5c=0, high byte &3==3 -> 63e58=1, &5==5 -> 63e5c=1 (**four sequential tests, the second pair wins only where its own mask matches**). DAT_63e4c = 0 then a bit search: while (0x40 >> (DAT_63e4c&0x1f)) != (short)block[4] it increments until the counter is no longer < 0xe. DAT_63e60 = block[0x10] (bytes/line), DAT_63e64 = block[0x12] (x res = the pitch every blit uses), DAT_63e68 = block[0x14] (y res), **DAT_531f4 = block[6] << 10** (640<<10 = 0xa0000) and DAT_531f8 = that-1. Free the data seg, INT 0x10 AX=0x4F02 BX=0x101 through fn_0041e51; reply AH==1 -> "\nMode Setting Error!!!\n" (DS 0x175a) + return 0, else return 1. New sys shim: sys_dos_seg_alloc/free (real-mode memory image) + sys_vesa_info/mode_info/set_mode with tunable fields. **FINDING: fn_0003f3be / fn_0003f410 are VBE Set Win A/B Position (INT 10h/4F05), not palette calls** -- DAT_63e58/63e5c are the window A/B selectors set here from ModeAttributes, and fn_03f462 calls fn_03f3be(ROW) per row: a 640x480 frame needs A0000 bank switching, an open item for the SDL video backend (the port has one 64 KiB window). 10 checks | f.c, f.h, sys.c, sys.h, test_f1.c |
